number of qubits\nna = 16  # cavity photon cutoff\nnj = N // 2  # spin dimension\n\n# Create operators\na = tensor(destroy(na), qeye(2**N))  # cavity annihilation\naz = tensor(qeye(na), jz(nj))  # spin z operator\nap = tensor(qeye(na), (jplus(nj) + jminus(nj)))  # spin raising+lowering\n\n# Common Lindblad operators:\n# Cavity loss: [a]\n# Local dephasing: [sigma_z for each qubit] (approximated by Jz)\n# Local pumping: [sigma_minus] (approximated by J_minus)\n# Local emission: [sigma_plus] (approximated by J_plus)\n# Collective pumping: [J_minus]\n# Collective emission: [J_plus]\n```\n\n## Steady-State Computation\n\n```python\nfrom qutip import steadystate\n\n# Create Hamiltonian\nH = omega0 * Jz + omegac * a_dag_a + g * (a_dag + a) * spin_ops\n\n# Define collapse operators (Lindblad operators)\nc_ops = [kappa * a, gamma_phi * Jz, ...]\n\n# Solve for steady state\nrho_ss = steadystate(H, c_ops)\n\n# Trace out spins to get cavity state\nrho_cavity = ptrace(rho_ss, 0)  # trace out everything except cavity mode\n```\n\n## Wigner Function\n\n```python\nfrom qutip import wigner\n\n# Calculate Wigner function on grid\nxvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\npvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\n\nW = wigner(rho_cavity, xvec, pvec)\n```\n\n## Complete Example Pattern\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\nfrom qutip import *\n\n# Parameters\nN = 4\nna = 16\nomega0 = omegac = 1.0\ng = 2.0 / np.sqrt(N)\nkappa = 1.0\nnj = N // 2\n\n# Operators\na = tensor(destroy(na), qeye(2**N))\nad = a.dag()\na_num = ad * a\nJz = tensor(qeye(na), jz(nj))\nJp = tensor(qeye(na), jplus(nj))\nJm = tensor(qeye(na), jminus(nj))\n\n# Hamiltonian\nH = omega0 * Jz + omegac * a_num + g * (ad + a) * (Jp + Jm)\n\n# Collapse operators\nc_ops = [np.sqrt(kappa) * a]  # cavity loss\n# Add other dissipation channels as needed\n\n# Steady state\nrho_ss = steadystate(H, c_ops)\n\n# Trace out spins\nrho_cavity = ptrace(rho_ss, 0)\n\n# Wigner function\nxvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\npvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\nW = wigner(rho_cavity, xvec, pvec)\n```\n\n## Important Notes\n\n1. **Operator Construction**: Always use `tensor()` to build operators in the full Hilbert space\n2. **Collapse Operators**: Normalize by square root of decay rate: `sqrt(gamma) * operator`\n3. **Dimension Ordering**: First dimension is cavity, remaining are spins\n4. **Steady State**: Use `steadystate()` for finding stationary solutions\n5. **Ptrace**: Use `ptrace(rho, [0])` to keep only cavity mode\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1089,"cache_read_input_tokens":18094,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1089,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"60f99c09-f713-4bfa-a97d-480bf6c97e42","uuid":"8643050d-3d6b-4378-8e8a-cfb6420560f1"}

number of qubits\nna = 16  # cavity photon cutoff\nnj = N // 2  # spin dimension\n\n# Create operators\na = tensor(destroy(na), qeye(2**N))  # cavity annihilation\naz = tensor(qeye(na), jz(nj))  # spin z operator\nap = tensor(qeye(na), (jplus(nj) + jminus(nj)))  # trace out everything except cavity mode\n```\n\n## Wigner Function\n\n```python\nfrom qutip import wigner\n\n# Calculate Wigner function on grid\nxvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\npvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\n\nW = wigner(rho_cavity, xvec, pvec)\n```\n\n## Complete Example Pattern\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\nfrom qutip import *\n\n# Parameters\nN = 4\nna = 16\nomega0 = omegac = 1.0\ng = 2.0 / np.sqrt(N)\nkappa = 1.0\nnj = N // 2\n\n# Operators\na = tensor(destroy(na), qeye(2**N))\nad = a.dag()\na_num = ad * a\nJz = tensor(qeye(na), jz(nj))\nJp = tensor(qeye(na), jplus(nj))\nJm = tensor(qeye(na), jminus(nj))\n\n# Hamiltonian\nH = omega0 * Jz + omegac * a_num + g * (ad + a) * (Jp + Jm)\n\n# Collapse operators\nc_ops = [np.sqrt(kappa) * a]  # cavity loss\n# Add other dissipation channels as needed\n\n# Steady state\nrho_ss = steadystate(H, c_ops)\n\n# Trace out spins\nrho_cavity = ptrace(rho_ss, 0)\n\n# Wigner function\nxvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\npvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\nW = wigner(rho_cavity, xvec, pvec)\n```\n\n## Important Notes\n\n1. **Operator Construction**: Always use `tensor()` to build operators in the full Hilbert space\n2. **Collapse Operators**: Normalize by square root of decay rate: `sqrt(gamma) * operator`\n3. **Dimension Ordering**: First dimension is cavity, remaining are spins\n4. **Steady State**: Use `steadystate()` for finding stationary solutions\n5. **Ptrace**: Use `ptrace(rho, [0])` to keep only cavity mode\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_017sVBitB1eUbPnQ8ufUZEBT","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01N9Cxqg2PU1ubGjbGLgBdoU","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/lindblad-master-equation.md","content":"# Lindblad Master Equation and Dissipation Channels\n\n## Overview\nThe Lindblad master equation describes the evolution of open quantum systems in contact with a thermal or non-thermal environment.\n\n## Lindblad Master Equation Form\n$$\\frac{d\\rho}{dt} = -\\frac{i}{\\hbar}[H, \\rho] + \\sum_i \\gamma_i \\left(L_i \\rho L_i^\\dagger - \\frac{1}{2}\\{L_i^\\dagger L_i, \\rho\\}\\right)$$\n\nWhere:\n- $H$: System Hamiltonian\n- $L_i$: Lindblad operators (jump operators)\n- $\\gamma_i$: Decay rates\n- $\\{A, B\\} = AB + BA$ (anticommutator)\n\n## QuTiP Implementation\n\nIn QuTiP, the collapse operators list represents the Lindblad operators:\n```python\nc_ops = [sqrt(gamma_1) * L_1, sqrt(gamma_2) * L_2, ...]\n```\n\n## Common Dissipation Channels for Dicke Model\n\n### 1. Cavity Loss\n**Physical meaning**: Photons escape from cavity\n**Rate**: $\\kappa$\n**Lindblad operator**: $L = a$ (cavity annihilation)\n```python\nc_ops.append(np.sqrt(kappa) * a)\n```\n\n### 2. Local Dephasing\n**Physical meaning**: Coherence loss between spin states (T₂ process)\n**Rate**: $\\gamma_\\phi$\n**Lindblad operator**: $L = \\sigma_z$ (or $J_z$ for collective)\n```python\n# Using spin operators - approximated by J_z / 2\nJz_dephasing = tensor(qeye(na), jz(nj)) / (nj)\nc_ops.append(np.sqrt(gamma_phi) * Jz_dephasing)\n```\n\n### 3. Local Pumping (Upward Transitions)\n**Physical meaning**: External pump drives spins to excited state (thermal bath or laser)\n**Rate**: $\\gamma_\\uparrow$\n**Lindblad operator**: $L = \\sigma_-$ (lowering operator, in rotating frame for excitation)\n```python\n# For collective pumping scaled by number\nJm = tensor(qeye(na), jminus(nj))\nc_ops.append(np.sqrt(gamma_uparrow) * Jm)\n```\n\n### 4. Local Emission (Downward Transitions)\n**Physical meaning**: Spontaneous emission or decay of excited spins\n**Rate**: $\\gamma_\\downarrow$\n**Lindblad operator**: $L = \\sigma_+$ (raising operator, represents transitions)\n```python\nJp = tensor(qeye(na), jplus(nj))\nc_ops.append(np.sqrt(gamma_downarrow) * Jp)\n```\n\n### 5. Collective Pumping\n**Physical meaning**: Collective pump affecting all N spins simultaneously\n**Rate**: $\\gamma_\\Uparrow$\n**Lindblad operator**: $L = J_-$\n```python\nJm = tensor(qeye(na), jminus(nj))\nc_ops.append(np.sqrt(gamma_Uparrow) * Jm)\n```\n\n### 6. Collective Emission\n**Physical meaning**: Collective decay through super-radiant channels\n**Rate**: $\\gamma_\\Downarrow$\n**Lindblad operator**: $L = J_+$\n```python\nJp = tensor(qeye(na), jplus(nj))\nc_ops.append(np.sqrt(gamma_Downarrow) * Jp)\n```\n\n## Important Considerations\n\n1. **Operator Normalization**: Collapse operators are NOT normalized; the decay rate is the prefactor\n   ```python\n   c_ops.append(np.sqrt(gamma) * operator)  # Correct\n   c_ops.append(operator)  # Wrong - loses decay rate info\n   ```\n\n2. **Lindblad Form**: QuTiP automatically handles the anticommutator term\n   - Only provide Lindblad operators $L_i$\n   - QuTiP computes: $L_i \\rho L_i^\\dagger - \\frac{1}{2}\\{L_i^\\dagger L_i, \\rho\\}$\n\n3. **Multiple Channels**: Simply add all Lindblad operators to the list\n   ```python\n   c_ops = [\n       np.sqrt(kappa) * a,           # cavity loss\n       np.sqrt(gamma_phi) * dephasing,\n       np.sqrt(gamma_down) * decay,\n       # ... more channels\n   ]\n   ```\n\n4. **Physical Interpretation**:\n   - **Dephasing**: Pure dephasing, no population transfer\n   - **Emission**: Population leaves the system\n   - **Pumping**: Population enters the system\n   - **Collective**: Affects superposition of all spins\n\n## Steady State Computation\n\n```python\nfrom qutip import steadystate\n\n# Solve Lindblad equation to steady state\nrho_ss = steadystate(H, c_ops, method='direct')  # or 'eigen'\n\n# Check if converged\nprint(f\"Steady state purity: {purity(rho_ss)}\")\n```\n\n## Verification\n\nTo verify your setup is correct:\n1. Check that Liouvillian is properly normalized\n2. Verify steady state satisfies: $L[\\rho_{ss}] = 0$\n3. Check purity decreases monotonically toward steady state\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1519,"cache_read_input_tokens":19183,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1519,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"60f99c09-f713-4bfa-a97d-480bf6c97e42","uuid":"f94e34b6-6976-4a50-b838-c01e227a44e9"}

Local Emission (Downward Transitions)\n**Physical meaning**: Spontaneous emission or decay of excited spins\n**Rate**: $\\gamma_\\downarrow$\n**Lindblad operator**: $L = \\sigma_+$ (raising operator, represents transitions)\n```python\nJp = tensor(qeye(na), jplus(nj))\nc_ops.append(np.sqrt(gamma_downarrow) * Jp)\n```\n\n### 5. Collective Emission\n**Physical meaning**: Collective decay through super-radiant channels\n**Rate**: $\\gamma_\\Downarrow$\n**Lindblad operator**: $L = J_+$\n```python\nJp = tensor(qeye(na), jplus(nj))\nc_ops.append(np.sqrt(gamma_Downarrow) * Jp)\n```\n\n## Important Considerations\n\n1. **Operator Normalization**: Collapse operators are NOT normalized; the decay rate is the prefactor\n   ```python\n   c_ops.append(np.sqrt(gamma) * operator)  # Correct\n   c_ops.append(operator)  # Wrong - loses decay rate info\n   ```\n\n2. **Lindblad Form**: QuTiP automatically handles the anticommutator term\n   - Only provide Lindblad operators $L_i$\n   - QuTiP computes: $L_i \\rho L_i^\\dagger - \\frac{1}{2}\\{L_i^\\dagger L_i, \\rho\\}$\n\n3. **Multiple Channels**: Simply add all Lindblad operators to the list\n   ```python\n   c_ops = [\n       np.sqrt(kappa) * a,           # cavity loss\n       np.sqrt(gamma_phi) * dephasing,\n       np.sqrt(gamma_down) * decay,\n       # ... more channels\n   ]\n   ```\n\n4. **Physical Interpretation**:\n   - **Dephasing**: Pure dephasing, no population transfer\n   - **Emission**: Population leaves the system\n   - **Pumping**: Population enters the system\n   - **Collective**: Affects superposition of all spins\n\n## Steady State Computation\n\n```python\nfrom qutip import steadystate\n\n# Solve Lindblad equation to steady state\nrho_ss = steadystate(H, c_ops, method='direct')  # or 'eigen'\n\n# Check if converged\nprint(f\"Steady state purity: {purity(rho_ss)}\")\n```\n\n## Verification\n\nTo verify your setup is correct:\n1. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_017gTVa22Et4TDx4PN2SvuFc","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01ArPciYKQn8eNycUMhvcBYQ","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/wigner-function-calculation.md","content":"# Wigner Function Calculation and Phase Space Analysis\n\n## Overview\nThe Wigner function is a quasi-probability distribution in phase space that provides a complete description of a quantum state. For oscillator states (like cavity fields), it's defined in the quadrature basis.\n\n## Mathematical Definition\n$$W(x,p) = \\frac{1}{\\pi} \\int_{-\\infty}^{\\infty} d\\xi \\, e^{i p \\xi} \\langle x - \\xi/2 | \\rho | x + \\xi/2 \\rangle$$\n\nWhere:\n- $x$: Position quadrature (related to $a + a^\\dagger$)\n- $p$: Momentum quadrature (related to $-i(a - a^\\dagger)$)\n- $\\rho$: Density matrix\n\n## QuTiP Implementation\n\n### Basic Wigner Function Calculation\n```python\nfrom qutip import wigner\nimport numpy as np\n\n# Define grid in phase space\nxvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\npvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\n\n# Calculate Wigner function\nW = wigner(rho_cavity, xvec, pvec)\n\n# W shape: (len(pvec), len(xvec))\n# W[i, j] = W(x[j], p[i])\n```\n\n### Grid Resolution\n```python\n# Fine grid for detailed structure\nx = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\np = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\n\n# Coarse grid for faster computation (debugging)\nx_coarse = np.linspace(-6, 6, 100)\np_coarse = np.linspace(-6, 6, 100)\n```\n\n### Properties of Wigner Functions\n\n1. **Normalization**:\n   $$\\int\\int \\frac{dx \\, dp}{2\\pi} W(x,p) = 1$$\n\n2. **Marginal Distributions**:\n   $$\\int dp \\, W(x,p) = \\langle x|\\rho|x \\rangle$$ (position distribution)\n   $$\\int dx \\, W(x,p) = \\langle p|\\rho|p \\rangle$$ (momentum distribution)\n\n3. **Pure vs Mixed States**:\n   - Pure states: $W(x,p) \\geq -1/\\pi$ (can be negative)\n   - Mixed states: more spread and less negative\n\n4. **Physical Interpretation**:\n   - Positive regions: classical-like behavior\n   - Negative regions: quantum signature (non-classical)\n   - Width: uncertainty in quadratures\n\n## Relation to Quadratures\n\nThe quadratures are related to ladder operators:\n$$X = \\frac{a + a^\\dagger}{\\sqrt{2}}$$ (Position)\n$$P = \\frac{-i(a - a^\\dagger)}{\\sqrt{2}}$$ (Momentum)\n\nIn QuTiP's convention:\n- $x = X/\\sqrt{2}$\n- $p = P/\\sqrt{2}$\n\n## Common Quantum States and Their Wigner Functions\n\n### Ground State (Vacuum)\n```python\nrho_vac = basis(na, 0) * basis(na, 0).dag()\nW_vac = wigner(rho_vac, xvec, pvec)\n# Gaussian profile centered at origin\n```\n\n### Coherent State\n```python\nrho_coh = coherent(na, alpha) * coherent(na, alpha).dag()\nW_coh = wigner(rho_coh, xvec, pvec)\n# Gaussian displaced to (alpha.real, alpha.imag)\n```\n\n### Squeezed State\n```python\nrho_sq = squeeze(na, xi) * basis(na, 0) * basis(na, 0).dag() * squeeze(na, xi).dag()\nW_sq = wigner(rho_sq, xvec, pvec)\n# Elliptical Gaussian\n```\n\n### Superposition States\n```python\n# Cat state (superposition of two coherent states)\nrho_cat = (coherent(na, 2) + coherent(na, -2)).unit()\nW_cat = wigner(rho_cat, xvec, pvec)\n# Two Gaussian peaks with interference fringes\n```\n\n## Practical Computation Tips\n\n### 1. Cavity Cutoff and Grid Size\n```python\n# Choose grid slightly larger than coherent state amplitude\nna = 16  # photon number\n# For displaced states with |alpha| ~ sqrt(na), use x,p ∈ [-6,6]\nxvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\npvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\n```\n\n### 2. For oscillator states (like cavity fields), it's defined in the quadrature basis.\n\n## Mathematical Definition\n$$W(x,p) = \\frac{1}{\\pi} \\int_{-\\infty}^{\\infty} d\\xi \\, e^{i p \\xi} \\langle x - \\xi/2 | \\rho | x + \\xi/2 \\rangle$$\n\nWhere:\n- $x$: Position quadrature (related to $a + a^\\dagger$)\n- $p$: Momentum quadrature (related to $-i(a - a^\\dagger)$)\n- $\\rho$: Density matrix\n\n## QuTiP Implementation\n\n### Basic Wigner Function Calculation\n```python\nfrom qutip import wigner\nimport numpy as np\n\n# Define grid in phase space\nxvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\npvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\n\n# Calculate Wigner function\nW = wigner(rho_cavity, xvec, pvec)\n\n# W shape: (len(pvec), len(xvec))\n# W[i, j] = W(x[j], p[i])\n```\n\n### Grid Resolution\n```python\n# Fine grid for detailed structure\nx = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\np = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\n\n# Coarse grid for faster computation (debugging)\nx_coarse = np.linspace(-6, 6, 100)\np_coarse = np.linspace(-6, 6, 100)\n```\n\n### Properties of Wigner Functions\n\n1. **Normalization**:\n   $$\\int\\int \\frac{dx \\, dp}{2\\pi} W(x,p) = 1$$\n\n2. **Marginal Distributions**:\n   $$\\int dp \\, W(x,p) = \\langle x|\\rho|x \\rangle$$ (position distribution)\n   $$\\int dx \\, W(x,p) = \\langle p|\\rho|p \\rangle$$ (momentum distribution)\n\n3. **Pure vs Mixed States**:\n   - Pure states: $W(x,p) \\geq -1/\\pi$ (can be negative)\n   - Mixed states: more spread and less negative\n\n4. **Physical Interpretation**:\n   - Positive regions: classical-like behavior\n   - Negative regions: quantum signature (non-classical)\n   - Width: uncertainty in quadratures\n\n## Relation to Quadratures\n\nThe quadratures are related to ladder operators:\n$$X = \\frac{a + a^\\dagger}{\\sqrt{2}}$$ (Position)\n$$P = \\frac{-i(a - a^\\dagger)}{\\sqrt{2}}$$ (Momentum)\n\nIn QuTiP's convention:\n- $x = X/\\sqrt{2}$\n- $p = P/\\sqrt{2}$\n\n## Common Quantum States and Their Wigner Functions\n\n### Ground State (Vacuum)\n```python\nrho_vac = basis(na, 0) * basis(na, 0).dag()\nW_vac = wigner(rho_vac, xvec, pvec)\n# Gaussian profile centered at origin\n```\n\n### Coherent State\n```python\nrho_coh = coherent(na, alpha) * coherent(na, alpha).dag()\nW_coh = wigner(rho_coh, xvec, pvec)\n# Gaussian displaced to (alpha.real, alpha.imag)\n```\n\n### Squeezed State\n```python\nrho_sq = squeeze(na, xi) * basis(na, 0) * basis(na, 0).dag() * squeeze(na, xi).dag()\nW_sq = wigner(rho_sq, xvec, pvec)\n# Elliptical Gaussian\n```\n\n### Superposition States\n```python\n# Cat state (superposition of two coherent states)\nrho_cat = (coherent(na, 2) + coherent(na, -2)).unit()\nW_cat = wigner(rho_cat, xvec, pvec)\n# Two Gaussian peaks with interference fringes\n```\n\n## Practical Computation Tips\n\n### 1. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_01R9rw66Ukr87AJfQTxWR3zF","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01LHfJLw4pQ4RHTbgaVxNgNY","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/dicke_model_simulation.py","content":"#!/usr/bin/env python\n\"\"\"\nOpen Dicke Model Simulation with Steady State Analysis\nCalculates Wigner functions for cavity field under different dissipation scenarios\n\"\"\"\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\nfrom qutip import *\nimport warnings\nwarnings.filterwarnings('ignore')\n\ndef setup_dicke_model(N=4, na=16):\n    \"\"\"\n    Set up the Dicke model Hamiltonian and basic operators.\n\n    Parameters:\n    -----------\n    N : int\n        Number of two-level systems (qubits)\n    na : int\n        Photon number cutoff for cavity mode\n\n    Returns:\n    --------\n    dict : Contains H, a, ad, Jz, Jp, Jm operators\n    \"\"\"\n    # Parameters\n    omega0 = 1.0\n    omegac = 1.0\n    g = 2.0 / np.sqrt(N)\n\n    # Hilbert space dimensions\n    nj = N // 2  # spin-N/2 representation\n\n    # Create operators in full Hilbert space (cavity + spins)\n    # Cavity operators\n    a = tensor(destroy(na), qeye(2**N))\n    ad = a.dag()\n    a_num = ad * a\n\n    # Spin operators (collective angular momentum)\n    Jz = tensor(qeye(na), jz(nj))\n    Jp = tensor(qeye(na), jplus(nj))\n    Jm = tensor(qeye(na), jminus(nj))\n\n    # Hamiltonian: H = ω₀ Jz + ωc a†a + g(a† + a)(J+ + J-)\n    H = omega0 * Jz + omegac * a_num + g * (ad + a) * (Jp + Jm)\n\n    return {\n        'H': H,\n        'a': a,\n        'ad': ad,\n        'Jz': Jz,\n        'Jp': Jp,\n        'Jm': Jm,\n        'g': g,\n        'omega0': omega0,\n        'omegac': omegac,\n        'N': N,\n        'na': na\n    }\n\ndef compute_case_1(model_dict, kappa=1.0, gamma_phi=0.01, gamma_up=0.1):\n    \"\"\"\n    Case 1: Local dephasing & local pumping\n    \"\"\"\n    H = model_dict['H']\n    a = model_dict['a']\n    Jz = model_dict['Jz']\n    Jm = model_dict['Jm']\n\n    # Collapse operators (Lindblad operators)\n    c_ops = [\n        np.sqrt(kappa) * a,           # Cavity loss\n        np.sqrt(gamma_phi) * Jz,       # Local dephasing\n        np.sqrt(gamma_up) * Jm         # Local pumping (J-)\n    ]\n\n    return H, c_ops\n\ndef compute_case_2(model_dict, kappa=1.0, gamma_phi=0.01, gamma_down=0.1):\n    \"\"\"\n    Case 2: Local dephasing & local emission\n    \"\"\"\n    H = model_dict['H']\n    a = model_dict['a']\n    Jz = model_dict['Jz']\n    Jp = model_dict['Jp']\n\n    # Collapse operators\n    c_ops = [\n        np.sqrt(kappa) * a,            # Cavity loss\n        np.sqrt(gamma_phi) * Jz,        # Local dephasing\n        np.sqrt(gamma_down) * Jp        # Local emission (J+)\n    ]\n\n    return H, c_ops\n\ndef compute_case_3(model_dict, kappa=1.0, gamma_phi=0.01, gamma_down=0.1, gamma_up_coll=0.1):\n    \"\"\"\n    Case 3: Local dephasing & local emission & collective pumping\n    \"\"\"\n    H = model_dict['H']\n    a = model_dict['a']\n    Jz = model_dict['Jz']\n    Jp = model_dict['Jp']\n    Jm = model_dict['Jm']\n\n    # Collapse operators\n    c_ops = [\n        np.sqrt(kappa) * a,            # Cavity loss\n        np.sqrt(gamma_phi) * Jz,        # Local dephasing\n        np.sqrt(gamma_down) * Jp,       # Local emission (J+)\n        np.sqrt(gamma_up_coll) * Jm     # Collective pumping (J-)\n    ]\n\n    return H, c_ops\n\ndef compute_case_4(model_dict, kappa=1.0, gamma_phi=0.01, gamma_down=0.1, gamma_down_coll=0.1):\n    \"\"\"\n    Case 4: Local dephasing & local emission & collective emission\n    \"\"\"\n    H = model_dict['H']\n    a = model_dict['a']\n    Jz = model_dict['Jz']\n    Jp = model_dict['Jp']\n\n    # Collapse operators (Lindblad operators)\n    c_ops = [\n        np.sqrt(kappa) * a,           # Cavity loss\n        np.sqrt(gamma_phi) * Jz,       # Local dephasing\n        np.sqrt(gamma_up) * Jm         # Collapse operators\n    c_ops = [\n        np.sqrt(kappa) * a,            # Cavity loss\n        np.sqrt(gamma_phi) * Jz,        # Local dephasing\n        np.sqrt(gamma_down) * Jp,       # Local emission (J+)\n        np.sqrt(gamma_up_coll) * Jm     # Collective emission (J+)\n    ]\n\n    return H, c_ops\n\ndef solve_steady_state_and_wigner(H, c_ops, model_dict):\n    \"\"\"\n    Solve for steady state, trace out spins, and compute Wigner function.\n\n    Parameters:\n    -----------\n    H : Qobj\n        Hamiltonian\n    c_ops : list\n        Collapse operators (Lindblad operators)\n    model_dict : dict\n        Contains model parameters and operators\n\n    Returns:\n    --------\n    W : ndarray\n        Wigner function (1000x1000 grid)\n    rho_cavity : Qobj\n        Cavity density matrix (reduced)\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"  Solving for steady state...\")\n\n    # Solve for steady state\n    rho_ss = steadystate(H, c_ops, method='direct')\n\n    # Trace out spins, keep only cavity (mode 0)\n    rho_cavity = ptrace(rho_ss, 0)\n\n    print(\"  Computing Wigner function on 1000x1000 grid...\")\n\n    # Create grid for Wigner function\n    xvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\n    pvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\n\n    # Compute Wigner function\n    W = wigner(rho_cavity, xvec, pvec)\n\n    print(f\"  Cavity state purity: {purity(rho_cavity):.6f}\")\n    print(f\"  Wigner function shape: {W.shape}\")\n\n    return W, rho_cavity\n\ndef save_wigner_to_csv(W, filename):\n    \"\"\"\n    Save Wigner function to CSV file.\n\n    The Wigner function is saved as a 2D array where:\n    - Rows correspond to momentum quadrature (p)\n    - Columns correspond to position quadrature (x)\n\n    Parameters:\n    -----------\n    W : ndarray\n        Wigner function (2D array)\n    filename : str\n        Output filename\n    \"\"\"\n    np.savetxt(filename, W, delimiter=',', fmt='%.10e')\n    print(f\"  Saved to {filename}\")\n\ndef main():\n    \"\"\"Main execution function\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\"*70)\n    print(\"Open Dicke Model Simulation - Wigner Function Analysis\")\n    print(\"=\"*70 + \"\\n\")\n\n    # Cavity annihilation operator in full space\n    a = tensor(destroy(na), *[qeye(2) for _ in range(N)])\n    ad = a.dag()\n    a_num = ad * a\n\n    # Pauli matrices for qubits\n    sz = np.array([[1, 0], [0, -1]])\n    sp = np.array([[0, 1], [0, 0]])\n    sm = np.array([[0, 0], [1, 0]])\n\n    sp_qobj = Qobj(sp, dims=[[2], [2]])\n    sm_qobj = Qobj(sm, dims=[[2], [2]])\n    sz_qobj = Qobj(sz, dims=[[2], [2]])\n\n    # Dicke Hamiltonian\n    H = omega0 * Jz + omegac * a_num + g * (ad + a) * (Jp + Jm)\n\n    return {\n        'H': H,\n        'a': a,\n        'ad': ad,\n        'Jz': Jz,\n        'Jp': Jp,\n        'Jm': Jm,\n        'g': g,\n        'N': N,\n        'na': na\n    }\n\ndef compute_steady_state_wigner(H, c_ops, na=16):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute steady state and Wigner function.\n\n    Parameters:\n    -----------\n    H : Qobj\n        Hamiltonian\n    c_ops : list\n        Collapse operators\n    na : int\n        Cavity photon cutoff\n\n    Returns:\n    --------\n    W : ndarray (1000, 1000)\n        Wigner function\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"    Solving for steady state...\")\n\n    # Grid for Wigner function\n    xvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\n    pvec = np.linspace(-6, 6, 1000)\n\n    # Compute Wigner function\n    W = wigner(rho_cavity, xvec, pvec)\n\n    return W\n\ndef main():\n    \"\"\"Main execution\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\"*75)\n    print(\"OPEN DICKE MODEL - WIGNER FUNCTION ANALYSIS\")\n    print(\"=\"*75)\n\n    # Cavity annihilation operator in full space\n    a = tensor(destroy(na), *[qeye(2) for _ in range(N)])\n    ad = a.dag()\n    a_num = ad * a\n\n    # Pauli matrices for qubits\n    sz = np.array([[1, 0], [0, -1]])\n    sp = np.array([[0, 1], [0, 0]])\n    sm = np.array([[0, 0], [1, 0]])\n\n    sp_qobj = Qobj(sp, dims=[[2], [2]])\n    sm_qobj = Qobj(sm, dims=[[2], [2]])\n    sz_qobj = Qobj(sz, dims=[[2], [2]])\n\n    # Build collective spin operators\n    Jp = 0  # raising operator (emission-like)\n    Jm = 0  # lowering operator (pumping-like)\n    Jz = 0  # z-component\n\n    for k in range(N):\n        # Operators for qubit k\n        ops_p = [qeye(na)] + [sp_qobj if i == k else qeye(2) for i in range(N)]\n        ops_m = [qeye(na)] + [sm_qobj if i == k else qeye(2) for i in range(N)]\n        ops_z = [qeye(na)] + [sz_qobj if i == k else qeye(2) for i in range(N)]\n\n        Jp += tensor(*ops_p)\n        Jm += tensor(*ops_m)\n        Jz += tensor(*ops_z) / 2.0\n\n    # Dicke Hamiltonian\n    H = omega0 * Jz + omegac * a_num + g * (ad + a) * (Jp + Jm)\n\n    return {\n        'H': H,\n        'a': a,\n        'ad': ad,\n        'Jz': Jz,\n        'Jp': Jp,\n        'Jm': Jm,\n        'g': g,\n        'N': N,\n        'na': na\n    }\n\ndef compute_steady_state_wigner(H, c_ops, na=16):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute steady state and Wigner function.\n\n    Parameters:\n    -----------\n    H : Qobj\n        Hamiltonian\n    c_ops : list\n        Collapse operators\n    na : int\n        Cavity photon cutoff\n\n    Returns:\n    --------\n    W : ndarray (1000, 1000)\n        Wigner function\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"    Solving for steady state...\")\n\n    #
